{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011CgNPgAgjUar1rSsZ2Bc7o","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-RowCounts.md ===\n# FEATURE: Row Counts\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Count data records, null values, and distinct non-null values for one column. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-NullValues.md, FEATURE-InputRejection.md |\n| Provides    | profile.Counts |\n| Consumes    | csvdata.IsNull |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nFor each column the profile reports `rows`, `nulls`, and `distinct`. This story implements the\ncounting function in `internal/profile`. Rendering the counts into the profile CSV belongs to the\nprofile output story.\n\n## Interface\n\n```go\n// Counts holds the three counts reported for one column.\ntype CountResult struct {\n    Rows     int\n    Nulls    int\n    Distinct int\n}\n\n// Counts takes every trimmed value of one column, in file order, and returns its counts.\nfunc Counts(values []string) CountResult\n```\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `Rows` is the number of data records, that is `len(values)`. A header-only file gives zero values\n  and therefore `Rows` 0.\n- `Nulls` is the number of values for which `csvdata.IsNull` is true (empty, `NA`, `N/A`, `NULL`\n  ignoring case).\n- `Distinct` is the number of different non-null values, compared as exact strings after trimming.\n  `Globex` and `globex` are different values. Null values are never counted as distinct.\n- A column whose values are all null has `Distinct` 0.\n\n## Required Tests\n\nThe project suite in `internal/profile` includes these tests by exact name, each written against\nreal output:\n\n- `TestCountsRowsNullsDistinct` — mixed null and non-null values give the expected three counts.\n- `TestCountsHeaderOnly` — no values give all three counts zero.\n- `TestCountsDistinctExactStrings` — values differing only in case are distinct; repeated values\n  count once.\n- `TestCountsAllNull` — a column of only null spellings has `Distinct` 0 and `Nulls` equal to `Rows`.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC rowcounts-rows-nulls-distinct ===\nIntent: Counts reports rows, nulls, and distinct for a mixed column.\nRequires: executable=go; The rule depends on the column type. This\nstory implements the extremes computation in `internal/profile`; two-decimal formatting of\n`number` results belongs to the decimal formatting story.\n\n## Interface\n\n```go\n// Extremes holds the minimum and maximum of one column.\ntype Extremes struct {\n    Min, Max       string   // text form, see below\n    MinRat, MaxRat *big.Rat // exact values for integer and number columns, else nil\n}\n\n// MinMax takes the column type and the trimmed non-null values of the column.\nfunc MinMax(typ string, values []string) Extremes\n```\n\n`typ` is one of `integer`, `number`, `date`, `boolean`, `text`, `empty`.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `integer`: compare exactly with `math/big`, never as `float64`. `Min` and `Max` are the smallest\n  and largest values written as plain integers. `MinRat` and `MaxRat` are set.\n- `number`: compare exactly as `big.Rat` (so `2.5` is less than `10.25`, and `-0.5` less than\n  `-0.25`). `MinRat` and `MaxRat` are set; `Min` and `Max` hold the source text of the extreme\n  values, and the decimal formatting story renders them with two places.\n- `date`: `Min` is the earliest and `Max` the latest. `YYYY-MM-DD` text orders chronologically.\n- `boolean`: `Min` is `false` if any value is false (ignoring case), else `true`. `Max` is `true`\n  if any value is true (ignoring case), else `false`. Output is lowercase whatever the input case.\n- `text`: smallest and largest by byte value, not locale collation. Upper case sorts before lower\n  case; a space sorts before letters.\n- `empty`: `Min` and `Max` are blank and the `Rat` fields are nil.\n- A column with exactly one non-null value reports that value as both minimum and maximum.\n\n## Required Tests\n\nThe project suite in `internal/profile` includes these tests by exact name:\n\n- `TestMinMaxInteger` — signed integers, including a negative, are ordered numerically, not\n  lexically (`9` is less than `10`).\n- `TestMinMaxNumber` — decimals are ordered exactly, including negatives and differing digit counts.\n- `TestMinMaxDate` — earliest and latest dates.\n- `TestMinMaxBoolean` — mixed case input yields lowercase output; all-true gives `true` for both;\n  all-false gives `false` for both; mixed gives `false` and `true`.\n- `TestMinMaxText` — byte-value ordering, including mixed case.\n- `TestMinMaxEmpty` — an empty column yields blank extremes.\n- `TestMinMaxSingleValue` — one value is both minimum and maximum.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC minmax-integer ===\nIntent: Integer extremes are ordered numerically with exact arithmetic.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestMinMaxInteger\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC minmax-integer ===\n\n=== AC minmax-number ===\nIntent: Number extremes are ordered exactly as decimals.\nRequires: executable=go; This story computes the exact values; formatting belongs to the\ndecimal formatting story.\n\n## Interface\n\n```go\n// SumMean takes the column type and the trimmed non-null values of the column. ok is false when\n// the type is not integer or number, or when there are no values.\nfunc SumMean(typ string, values []string) (sum, mean *big.Rat, ok bool)\n```\n\n## Behavior\n\n- Only `integer` and `number` columns have a sum and mean. Null values are neither summed nor\n  counted.\n- The returned values are exact rationals. They are not rounded here.\n\n## Required Tests\n\nThe project suite in `internal/profile` includes these tests by exact name:\n\n- `TestSumIntegerExact` — an integer total, including negatives, equals the big-integer total.\n- `TestSumNumberNoFloatDrift` — decimals such as `0.1` and `0.2` sum exactly to their rational total.\n- `TestMeanExactRational` — total over count is an exact rational, for example a repeating third.\n- `TestSumMeanIgnoresNulls` — the divisor is the non-null count, not the row count.\n- `TestSumMeanNonNumeric` — text, date, boolean, and empty columns give `ok` false.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC summean-integer-sum ===\nIntent: Integer sums are exact, including negative values.\nRequires: executable=go; Rounding is done on the exact rational with integer\n  arithmetic, never through `float64` or `fmt` rounding of a float.\n- Exactly two decimal places are always written, so a whole value has `.00`.\n- A leading `-` is written only when the rounded result is non-zero. A value that rounds to zero\n  is written `0.00` with no minus sign.\n- No thousands separator is written.\n- Very large magnitudes keep every digit.\n- `FormatInteger` writes the integer with no decimal point and no separator, with `-` for negatives\n  and no negative zero.\n- Which statistics use which function: `number` min, max, sum, mean, and median, and `integer`\n  mean and median use `FormatDecimal`; `integer` min, max, and sum use `FormatInteger`. `date`,\n  `boolean`, `text` extremes are written as given.\n\n## Required Tests\n\nThe project suite in `internal/profile` includes these tests by exact name:\n\n- `TestFormatDecimalTwoPlaces` — whole, one-place, and many-place values all gain exactly two places.\n- `TestFormatDecimalHalfAwayFromZero` — positive and negative exact ties round away from zero.\n- `TestFormatDecimalNoNegativeZero` — a small negative value that rounds to zero is `0.00`.\n- `TestFormatDecimalNoSeparator` — a value above one thousand has no thousands separator.\n- `TestFormatDecimalLargeExact` — a magnitude beyond `float64` precision keeps all digits.\n- `TestFormatInteger` — plain integers, negatives, and zero.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC decfmt-two-places ===\nIntent: FormatDecimal always writes exactly two decimal places.\nRequires: executable=go; distinct non-null values for one column. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-NullValues.md, FEATURE-InputRejection.md |\n| Provides    | profile.Counts |\n| Consumes    | csvdata.IsNull |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nFor each column the profile reports `rows`, `nulls`, and `distinct`. This story implements the\ncounting function in `internal/profile`. Rendering the counts into the profile CSV belongs to the\nprofile output story.\n\n## Interface\n\n```go\n// Counts holds the three counts reported for one column.\ntype CountResult struct {\n    Rows     int\n    Nulls    int\n    Distinct int\n}\n\n// Counts takes every trimmed value of one column, in file order, and returns its counts.\nfunc Counts(values []string) CountResult\n```\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `Rows` is the number of data records, that is `len(values)`. A header-only file gives zero values\n  and therefore `Rows` 0.\n- `Nulls` is the number of values for which `csvdata.IsNull` is true (empty, `NA`, `N/A`, `NULL`\n  ignoring case).\n- `Distinct` is the number of different non-null values, compared as exact strings after trimming.\n  `Globex` and `globex` are different values. Null values are never counted as distinct.\n- A column whose values are all null has `Distinct` 0.\n\n## Required Tests\n\nThe project suite in `internal/profile` includes these tests by exact name, each written against\nreal output:\n\n- `TestCountsRowsNullsDistinct` — mixed null and non-null values give the expected three counts.\n- `TestCountsHeaderOnly` — no values give all three counts zero.\n- `TestCountsDistinctExactStrings` — values differing only in case are distinct; repeated values\n  count once.\n- `TestCountsAllNull` — a column of only null spellings has `Distinct` 0 and `Nulls` equal to `Rows`.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC rowcounts-rows-nulls-distinct ===\nIntent: Counts reports rows, nulls, and distinct for a mixed column.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestCountsRowsNullsDistinct\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC rowcounts-rows-nulls-distinct ===\n\n=== AC rowcounts-header-only ===\nIntent: A column with no data records reports zero for every count.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestCountsHeaderOnly\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC rowcounts-header-only ===\n\n=== AC rowcounts-distinct-exact ===\nIntent: Distinct compares exact strings and counts repeats once.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestCountsDistinctExactStrings\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC rowcounts-distinct-exact ===\n\n=== AC rowcounts-all-null ===\nIntent: An all-null column has zero distinct values and every row null.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestCountsAllNull\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC rowcounts-all-null ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Counts are computed from the values given; no count is embedded or read from `sources/`.\n- Distinct never merges values by case folding or further trimming.\n- Application code never reads `sources/run.sh`.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-MinMax.md ===\n# FEATURE: Min and Max\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Compute the minimum and maximum of a column according to its inferred type. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-BooleanTextTypes.md |\n| Provides    | profile.MinMax |\n| Consumes    | profile.InferType |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nThe profile reports `min` and `max` for every column. The rule depends on the column type. This\nstory implements the extremes computation in `internal/profile`; two-decimal formatting of\n`number` results belongs to the decimal formatting story.\n\n## Interface\n\n```go\n// Extremes holds the minimum and maximum of one column.\ntype Extremes struct {\n    Min, Max       string   // text form, see below\n    MinRat, MaxRat *big.Rat // exact values for integer and number columns, else nil\n}\n\n// MinMax takes the column type and the trimmed non-null values of the column.\nfunc MinMax(typ string, values []string) Extremes\n```\n\n`typ` is one of `integer`, `number`, `date`, `boolean`, `text`, `empty`.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `integer`: compare exactly with `math/big`, never as `float64`. `Min` and `Max` are the smallest\n  and largest values written as plain integers. `MinRat` and `MaxRat` are set.\n- `number`: compare exactly as `big.Rat` (so `2.5` is less than `10.25`, and `-0.5` less than\n  `-0.25`). `MinRat` and `MaxRat` are set; `Min` and `Max` hold the source text of the extreme\n  values, and the decimal formatting story renders them with two places.\n- `date`: `Min` is the earliest and `Max` the latest. `YYYY-MM-DD` text orders chronologically.\n- `boolean`: `Min` is `false` if any value is false (ignoring case), else `true`. `Max` is `true`\n  if any value is true (ignoring case), else `false`. Output is lowercase whatever the input case.\n- `text`: smallest and largest by byte value, not locale collation. Upper case sorts before lower\n  case; a space sorts before letters.\n- `empty`: `Min` and `Max` are blank and the `Rat` fields are nil.\n- A column with exactly one non-null value reports that value as both minimum and maximum.\n\n## Required Tests\n\nThe project suite in `internal/profile` includes these tests by exact name:\n\n- `TestMinMaxInteger` — signed integers, including a negative, are ordered numerically, not\n  lexically (`9` is less than `10`).\n- `TestMinMaxNumber` — decimals are ordered exactly, including negatives and differing digit counts.\n- `TestMinMaxDate` — earliest and latest dates.\n- `TestMinMaxBoolean` — mixed case input yields lowercase output; all-true gives `true` for both;\n  all-false gives `false` for both; mixed gives `false` and `true`.\n- `TestMinMaxText` — byte-value ordering, including mixed case.\n- `TestMinMaxEmpty` — an empty column yields blank extremes.\n- `TestMinMaxSingleValue` — one value is both minimum and maximum.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC minmax-integer ===\nIntent: Integer extremes are ordered numerically with exact arithmetic.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestMinMaxInteger\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC minmax-integer ===\n\n=== AC minmax-number ===\nIntent: Number extremes are ordered exactly as decimals.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestMinMaxNumber\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC minmax-number ===\n\n=== AC minmax-date ===\nIntent: Date extremes are the earliest and latest dates.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestMinMaxDate\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC minmax-date ===\n\n=== AC minmax-boolean ===\nIntent: Boolean extremes follow the false/true rule and are lowercase regardless of input case.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestMinMaxBoolean\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC minmax-boolean ===\n\n=== AC minmax-text ===\nIntent: Text extremes are ordered by byte value.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestMinMaxText\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC minmax-text ===\n\n=== AC minmax-empty ===\nIntent: An empty column has blank minimum and maximum.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestMinMaxEmpty\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC minmax-empty ===\n\n=== AC minmax-single-value ===\nIntent: A single non-null value is both the minimum and the maximum.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestMinMaxSingleValue\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC minmax-single-value ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Numeric comparison never uses `float64`.\n- Text comparison is by byte value only.\n- No extreme is embedded in code or read from `sources/`; every value comes from the input given.\nEOF_NOT_USED\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-SumMean.md ===\n# FEATURE: Sum and Mean\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Compute the exact sum and mean of integer and number columns. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-BooleanTextTypes.md |\n| Provides    | profile.SumMean |\n| Consumes    | profile.InferType |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nNumeric columns report `sum` and `mean`. Exactness matters because the output is rounded once, at\nwrite time, from the exact value. This story computes the exact values; formatting belongs to the\ndecimal formatting story.\n\n## Interface\n\n```go\n// SumMean takes the column type and the trimmed non-null values of the column. ok is false when\n// the type is not integer or number, or when there are no values.\nfunc SumMean(typ string, values []string) (sum, mean *big.Rat, ok bool)\n```\n\n## Behavior\n\n- Only `integer` and `number` columns have a sum and mean. For every other type, including `empty`,\n  `ok` is false.\n- Parse every value into a `big.Rat` and accumulate exactly with `math/big`. No `float64` is used\n  at any step, so `0.1` plus `0.2` is exactly three tenths.\n- `sum` is the exact total of the non-null values.\n- `mean` is the exact total divided by the non-null count. Null values are neither summed nor\n  counted.\n- The returned values are exact rationals. They are not rounded here.\n\n## Required Tests\n\nThe project suite in `internal/profile` includes these tests by exact name:\n\n- `TestSumIntegerExact` — an integer total, including negatives, equals the big-integer total.\n- `TestSumNumberNoFloatDrift` — decimals such as `0.1` and `0.2` sum exactly to their rational total.\n- `TestMeanExactRational` — total over count is an exact rational, for example a repeating third.\n- `TestSumMeanIgnoresNulls` — the divisor is the non-null count, not the row count.\n- `TestSumMeanNonNumeric` — text, date, boolean, and empty columns give `ok` false.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC summean-integer-sum ===\nIntent: Integer sums are exact, including negative values.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestSumIntegerExact\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC summean-integer-sum ===\n\n=== AC summean-number-no-drift ===\nIntent: Decimal sums carry no floating-point drift.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestSumNumberNoFloatDrift\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC summean-number-no-drift ===\n\n=== AC summean-mean-exact ===\nIntent: The mean is the exact total divided by the non-null count.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestMeanExactRational\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC summean-mean-exact ===\n\n=== AC summean-ignores-nulls ===\nIntent: The mean divisor is the non-null count.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestSumMeanIgnoresNulls\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC summean-ignores-nulls ===\n\n=== AC summean-non-numeric ===\nIntent: Non-numeric column types have no sum or mean.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestSumMeanNonNumeric\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC summean-non-numeric ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- No `float64` arithmetic in sum or mean.\n- Values are rounded once, at output time, never here.\n- No total is embedded in code or read from `sources/`.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-Median.md ===\n# FEATURE: Median\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Compute the exact median of integer and number columns. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-SumMean.md |\n| Provides    | profile.Median |\n| Consumes    | profile.SumMean |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nNumeric columns report a `median`. It is computed exactly and rounded once at output time by the\ndecimal formatting story.\n\n## Interface\n\n```go\n// Median takes the column type and the trimmed non-null values of the column. ok is false when\n// the type is not integer or number, or when there are no values.\nfunc Median(typ string, values []string) (median *big.Rat, ok bool)\n```\n\n## Behavior\n\n- Parse values into `big.Rat` and sort them numerically (`9` before `10`, `-3` before `2.5`),\n  never lexically.\n- With an odd count the median is the middle value.\n- With an even count the median is the exact mean of the two middle values.\n- Null values are excluded before counting.\n- Non-numeric types and columns with no non-null value give `ok` false.\n- The input slice is not reordered for the caller.\n\n## Required Tests\n\nThe project suite in `internal/profile` includes these tests by exact name:\n\n- `TestMedianOddCount` — the middle of an unsorted odd-length list.\n- `TestMedianEvenCountExact` — the exact mean of the two middle values, including a case whose\n  result is not representable in binary floating point.\n- `TestMedianNumericOrder` — numeric rather than lexical ordering.\n- `TestMedianIgnoresNulls` — nulls are excluded from the count.\n- `TestMedianNonNumeric` — non-numeric and empty columns give `ok` false.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC median-odd ===\nIntent: An odd count yields the middle value of the sorted values.\nRequires: executable=go; This story provides the formatting functions the\nprofile output story applies to each statistic.\n\n## Interface\n\n```go\n// FormatDecimal writes r with exactly two decimal places.\nfunc FormatDecimal(r *big.Rat) string\n\n// FormatInteger writes an integer-valued r as a plain integer.\nfunc FormatInteger(r *big.Rat) string\n```\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `FormatDecimal` rounds the exact value to two places, half away from zero: an exact tie such as\n  one thousandth short of a half cent rounds by magnitude upward for both signs (`0.005` becomes\n  `0.01`, `-0.005` becomes `-0.01`). Rounding is done on the exact rational with integer\n  arithmetic, never through `float64` or `fmt` rounding of a float.\n- Exactly two decimal places are always written, so a whole value has `.00`.\n- A leading `-` is written only when the rounded result is non-zero. A value that rounds to zero\n  is written `0.00` with no minus sign.\n- No thousands separator is written.\n- Very large magnitudes keep every digit.\n- `FormatInteger` writes the integer with no decimal point and no separator, with `-` for negatives\n  and no negative zero.\n- Which statistics use which function: `number` min, max, sum, mean, and median, and `integer`\n  mean and median use `FormatDecimal`; `integer` min, max, and sum use `FormatInteger`. `date`,\n  `boolean`, `text` extremes are written as given.\n\n## Required Tests\n\nThe project suite in `internal/profile` includes these tests by exact name:\n\n- `TestFormatDecimalTwoPlaces` — whole, one-place, and many-place values all gain exactly two places.\n- `TestFormatDecimalHalfAwayFromZero` — positive and negative exact ties round away from zero.\n- `TestFormatDecimalNoNegativeZero` — a small negative value that rounds to zero is `0.00`.\n- `TestFormatDecimalNoSeparator` — a value above one thousand has no thousands separator.\n- `TestFormatDecimalLargeExact` — a magnitude beyond `float64` precision keeps all digits.\n- `TestFormatInteger` — plain integers, negatives, and zero.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC decfmt-two-places ===\nIntent: FormatDecimal always writes exactly two decimal places.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestFormatDecimalTwoPlaces\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC decfmt-two-places ===\n\n=== AC decfmt-half-away ===\nIntent: Exact ties round half away from zero for both signs.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestFormatDecimalHalfAwayFromZero\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC decfmt-half-away ===\n\n=== AC decfmt-no-negative-zero ===\nIntent: A negative value that rounds to zero is written without a minus sign.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestFormatDecimalNoNegativeZero\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC decfmt-no-negative-zero ===\n\n=== AC decfmt-no-separator ===\nIntent: Large values are written without a thousands separator.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestFormatDecimalNoSeparator\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC decfmt-no-separator ===\n\n=== AC decfmt-large-exact ===\nIntent: Values beyond float64 precision keep every digit.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestFormatDecimalLargeExact\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC decfmt-large-exact ===\n\n=== AC decfmt-integer ===\nIntent: FormatInteger writes plain integers with no decimal point.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestFormatInteger\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC decfmt-integer ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Rounding happens once, on the exact rational, never through `float64`.\n- No negative zero in any output.\n- No thousands separator.\n- No formatted value is embedded in code or read from `sources/`.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===","ttft_ms":20624,"type":"result","duration_ms":66761,"uuid":"8f0072aa-e184-4cec-bbdb-4cf935e8428d","ttft_stream_ms":2710,"time_to_request_ms":95,"first_content_frame_ms":2710,"queued_turn_count":0,"result_index":0,"model_usage_since_last_result":{"claude-sonnet-5-5":{"inputTokens":2,"outputTokens":11111,"cacheReadInputTokens":1568,"cacheCreationInputTokens":76513,"webSearchRequests":0,"costUSD":0.4173228,"contextWindow":1000000,"maxOutputTokens":128000,"thinkingTokens":2349,"canonicalModel":"claude-sonnet-5-5","provider":"firstParty","costBasis":"list"}}}
